Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: How can one do Tax-loss harvesting on index funds? Large 100%+ capital gains, not-married, no property or loan

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →



But then funding for that 30k for doing the "short sale of Nvda for $30k" and investing that 30k in Intel is coming from borrowing from a margin loan on which you have to pay interest%, right?
So building these 2 positions (one long and one short) is coming at an extra expense even though overall market exposure (say VTI) is still 100%, if I understand correctly. But then how do we ensure that 2 stocks we pick (here nvda and intc) are fully correlated with the overall market like VTI as just 2 stocks almost definitely wouldn't be. How is this different from direct indexing as we are just trying to pick stocks that overall correlate with the market?

also the loss-harvesting on the position that generates loss has to be strong enough to offset the extra % interest that needs to be paid on the margin loan to create the $30k short sale, true?
First, no margin. Your are selling short a stock. I sell X shares on Nvida (that I don’t own). I take the money from the sale and reinvest my in Intel.

This is kind of, but not exactly, the same as direct indexing. To do direct investing you start with a pile of cash. For you to do that you would need to sell your VTI which would trigger capital gains.

Do one could leverage one’s portfolio to generate that cash. i.e. margin with margin interest rates. Now a portfolio that was 130% long stocks and 30% short stocks should perform the same as a 100% portfolio.

Lastly, you don’t want stocks with a high correlation to the market. What you really want are high beta(volatile) low correlation stocks. Now the odd thing is that a collection of these high beta low correlation stocks should have a high correlation to the overall market.

At least in theory. And the statistics suggest you will come out on top 87% of the time.

It is solid. I have seen it in action. It is complex.
Also to avoid literal "shorting against the box" regulation is it allowed to build a 130/30 long/short extension by building 30% long position using total-market ETF like VTI and 30% short using some other s&p 500 etf index-fund (not exactly total-market) like VOO that's not vti but heavily correlates with it (correlation chart https://stockanalysis.com/etf/compare/voo-vs-vti/)?

Photographer Jessica Craig-Martin’s New Memoir Reveals the Life Behind Her “Drunken Lens”

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Her new memoir I Regret I Am Able to Attend is out now

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Since the early ‘90s, photographer Jessica Craig-Martin has captured high society from unusual angles. She zooms in on air kisses and other mannered gestures, cleavage in couture gowns, high heels and the floors typically omitted from pristine event documentation, straddling the line between fashion photography and art portraiture.

Craig-Martin’s memoir, I Regret I Am Able to Attend, out now, tells the story of how she got there. Craig-Martin details her bohemian childhood, wild friendships, and volatile young marriage with the effervescence that fills her compositions.

The story begins with the photographer’s parents, artists Michael Craig-Martin and Jan Hashey, who meet at the Yale School of Art. They have Jessica, then swiftly separate after revelations that they’re both having affairs, and Michael comes out as gay. From there, Craig-Martin experiences a peripatetic childhood, eventually settling into her mother’s SoHo loft. Her creative milieu includes gallerist Paula Cooper and artists Elizabeth Murray, Jennifer Bartlett, and Joel Shapiro, along with their children. Craig-Martin gets a job at high-end grocer Dean and Deluca (RIP), where Basquiat comes in to buy smoked eel for a strange supper. Artists Brice and Helen Marden hire her to babysit their daughters, and soon she’s off to Morocco and Greece with the family.

Instead of attending college, Craig-Martin receives an alternative education as she lands a gig waitressing at Brian McNally’s celebrity-filled restaurant Indochine. She flies to London to start a job as a fashion assistant at Anna Wintour’s British Vogue (they love her American enthusiasm) and, back in New York, works at Tina Brown’s Vanity Fair (they love her British sophistication). Throughout the memoir, Craig-Martin captures the colorful characters who parade in and out of her life, and around a rapidly evolving New York City.

Ahead of the book’s publication, and her forthcoming show at the Andres Thalmann gallery in Paris, Bazaar spoke to Craig-Martin about her writing process and her shifting understanding of gender in the art world. We were on the telephone, and the artist was visiting “I Am Plane Image,” a show of Brice Marden’s paintings at Gagosian in Chelsea. A guard reprimanded her for getting too close to a painting. “When I was a kid, one was encouraged to touch the art,” she told me. “A lot of art was activated by the viewer. And now someone’s always saying: ‘Excuse me!’ ‘Excuse me!’”

You’re a society photographer whose pictures conjure an entire milieu. Your book does something similar, but with language. How do you think about the relationship between writing and photography?

It’s the same brain, making different things. With photography, I never thought about what I was going to make before I made it. I’d see something with my camera, and I’d have to grab it. It’s a wonderful feeling of adrenaline, and it’s similar to looking for the next word. Everything just disappears. There’s a loss of self in the process, and you become your purest self.

In the book, your aesthetic education seems to come, in part, from painter Brice Marden.

With the camera, I felt a certain freedom. I really didn’t know much about photography, because it wasn’t a popular medium with my parents and their friends. And from Brice, I understood this freedom to put what you want in a picture. You can leave things out, push it around until it works, make it as weird as you want. If you feel like cutting off the top of a head in a photograph, try it!

I knew certain photographers, but not society photographers like Tina Barney and Larry Fink. I only knew a few pictures of Weegee’s. There was a lot for me to discover. If I’d seen more of this work, I would have felt inadequate. In this case, ignorance was bliss. Academic training can make you self-conscious. I didn’t have anything to unlearn.

Your mother has a philosophy that artists are the real aristocracy. Do you share it

She was right about everything she said. But it is extreme. I mean, we want doctors and we need other professions. But I think all artists believe this, as did all of us art brats who grew up in this kind of crazy, free-range situation. Art comes first. Everything else comes afterwards. It’s what we are in the truest form.

Good work rises to the top. Then again, not everyone whose work is good has been correctly honored or addressed. Women and people of color were left out of the discourse.

Your mother also says that Indochine is where you became a photographer.

I was raised in the very lean 60s and 70s, and then suddenly New York just was drunk on money overnight. And this job threw me into the middle of it. It was a hot seat to view all this excess. I found my subject matter there. Everything’s moving at once, and I keep that feeling when I’m taking a photograph: there’s a lot of motion in my pictures, and I’m capturing the feet or I’m above the person or I’m not where a photographer would normally be. [Gallerist] Jeanne Greenberg calls it my “drunken lens.”

The book is also a portrait of an evolving New York. It’s more sanitized now.

New York is still New York. We lose a lot with the corporatization of the city. But that’s global. It’s the same fuckers in every city making it into glass, concrete, steel with a bank in the bottom of the building and a Starbucks. It’s more painful here because New York is my city. But you can’t be too nostalgic because it’s also about speed and change. If you get stuck, you’re missing the point.

The sex appeal is now in Brooklyn. I’m an old fart now, I don’t hang out, but I know there are young people who are opening galleries, making good art. It’s just harder and harder to find a crack in the sidewalk to grow out of.

Towards the end of the book, during your marriage to artist Ashley Bickerton, you write that you made “Art Wife Curry.” That comes shortly after your mention of Rirkrit Tiravanija making curry at 303 Gallery, which he just calls “art.” You write about the covert sexism in the art world vs. the overt sexism in the restaurant world. In the writing process, did you come to think any differently about how gender had shaped you?

Yeah. When you’re naive and you’re a young woman, you’re experiencing these invisible forces pushing you around, or men are behaving in a certain way, and you’re used to it. It’s like air. You don’t see it. And not just writing the book, but getting older, you can really see the forces that pulled you this way or that, where you felt a limitation. When I look back, I think: Why didn’t I just say “fuck you?” Or why didn’t I say, no no, I’m coming through? Women second guess themselves a great deal more than men. Men push and then afterwards say oh, did I break it? And then everyone claps. That’s not the classic woman’s story. There are corrections happening, and many of them are very well-deserved.

What kinds of limitations are you thinking about?

It’s about confidence. Being an unquestioning force in the world and feeling like you have a right to be seen on the wall, have just as big a studio as your husband or partner. If you go to the Krasner Pollock home in Springs in Long Island, his studio was famously the barn, a giant empty space. And hers was this tiny room. That would be the normal order of things. That’s the way it was in my family, even though I thought of my parents as liberated. My mother was considered a star at Yale, and as soon as she left and had a baby, she was a mother first, artist second. My parents’ generation thought they had dealt with a lot of this second classing of women, and they really hadn’t.

The book ends when you're around 35, your marriage is over, and your photography career has finally begun. Why end there? Will there be a Volume 2?

Good question. There might be. But I’ve also told my friends to tie me up and duct tape me to a wall if I ever start to try, because this was so difficult to make. I’m already forgetting how dreadful I found the experience.

'One to Watch' - Ben Jacobs: Man Utd Could Move to Sign Gusto for Pochettino

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester United could potentially be one of the busiest clubs in the January transfer window as they look to give new manager Mauricio Pochettino what he needs to turn the club's season around, with GIVEMESPORT senior correspondent Ben Jacobs naming a France international as one possible target.

Hopes were high of another strong season for Man United under the permanent leadership of Michael Carrick after their rise up the Premier League table in the first half of 2026, but instead they have made a miserable start to the campaign.

The Red Devils have won just one of their opening 10 Premier League matches this term, while losing three of their five fixtures in the EFL Cup and Champions League too, which has left them in the relegation places in the top flight.

It could get even worse for them this weekend, as they could be bottom of the table by the time they kick off in Pochettino's first match against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday afternoon.

That will happen if Crystal Palace win in Thomas Frank's first game in charge against Coventry City, and Sunderland draw with Aston Villa.

Pochettino knows he has a big task ahead to turn United's fortunes around, and especially from a defensive point of view, where they have conceded four goals against Como and Aston Villa, and three against Bournemouth, in their previous five fixtures.

GIVEMESPORT sources have revealed that club chiefs will look at signing a new striker in January, with Leeds United's Dominic Calvert-Lewin one player they are keen on, although the England international looks likely to sign a new contract at Elland Road instead.

Meanwhile, in his latest GMS newsletter, Jacobs has confirmed that United are also looking at the centre-back and full-back positions, with Chelsea's Malo Gusto 'one to watch'.

On the right hand side, even though maybe a right-sided full-back is not as high a priority as a left-sided one, Malo Gusto could be one to watch.

"There’s been no contact between Manchester United and Gusto yet, and Manchester City have looked in the past as well. But Chelsea are prepared to sell at the right price.

“Over the summer, that number was £75million and it would have to be dropped significantly, you would imagine anyway, if anyone’s going to come in for the France International, but more broadly, Gusto’s future is one to watch in this January window."

While United may not want to pay £75m to sign Gusto, especially given Chelsea are willing to sell him if the price is right, a move for the 23-year-old would also make a lot of sense.

The France international has already played under Pochettino, with the duo together at Stamford Bridge from the summer of 2023 to when the Argentine was sacked in May 2024.

Gusto knows how the 54-year-old wants to play and should be able to make an immediate impact in a Pochettino team as a result, while he also boasts 100 Premier League appearances and Champions League experience too.

Most impressive though is Gusto's versatility, as while his primary position is as a right-back or right wing-back, he has also been used as a left-back and even in midfield during his career.

It's no secret that United were looking for left-back competition for Luke Shaw in the summer, having shown strong interest in Newcastle United's Lewis Hall and Barcelona's Alejandro Balde, although neither move materialised as they prioritised signing three central midfielders.

Signing Gusto could kill two birds with one stone if he is seen as a player who can perform well in both roles, although there will of course be some caution given Diogo Dalot is a player who can play left-back and right-back, but has largely ultimately struggled when deployed on his weaker side.

Gusto has 17 caps for France and a big future ahead, and if United were able to secure him for a lower fee than the summer asking price, it could be a smart addition to help Pochettino.

Manchester United chiefs are ready to back Mauricio Pochettino in the transfer market

Leeds Ditched Star Labelled ‘Worse Than Augustin’, Now He’s Scored 11 CL Goals

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Leeds United have made some excellent signings in recent years, none more so than current club captain Ethan Ampadu, as well as England duo James Trafford and Dominic Calvert-Lewin.

Ampadu has taken the jump from the Championship to the Premier League like a duck to water, while Trafford and Calvert-Lewin both have the quality to take Leeds to the next level after they consolidated their spot in the top flight in the first season after promotion.

Calvert-Lewin is even attracting interest from rivals Manchester United but, according to GIVEMESPORT sources, he is set to extend his contract to stay at Elland Road instead.

It's fair to say that, like many clubs, the Yorkshire outfit haven't always got it right with their transfer business.

There are already big doubts over summer addition Jean-Matteo Bahoya, who was viewed as an exciting signing but who has barely been picked by Daniel Farke, with the club already making a decision on terminating his loan spell in the January transfer window.

Bahoya isn't the only forward to be a flop, so far at least, at Elland Road in recent years, with Facundo Buonanotte and Jean-Kevin Augustin two more prime examples.

Leeds signed the former on loan from Brighton and Hove Albion in January of this year to boost their survival battle, but he played just 36 minutes of Premier League football between then and the end of the campaign, before returning to the Seagulls.

The story of Augustin is even more interesting, from a player who signed on loan from RB Leipzig, to one who ended up costing the Whites around £40m, despite playing only 48 minutes of football for them.

Leeds signed Augustin on loan with a £18m obligation to buy the player if they won promotion to the Premier League, although they argued that, because the Championship season had been interrupted due to the coronavirus pandemic and not completed until July, Augustin's contract should have reverted to Leipzig at the time they actually went up.

Leeds lost their appeal and agreed on a £15.5m compensation package with Leipzig, while they were also ordered to pay Augustin himself £24.5m in compensation because of the failed permanent transfer.

Augustin will undoubtedly go down as Leeds' worst ever signing for that reason and the number of minutes paid, but Leeds content creator Joe Wainman told GIVEMESPORT previously that Weston McKennie was 'arguably worse than Augustin'.

He said: "The mad thing is, I'm that vitriolic towards last season and the demise of the football club, with the poor acquisitions that we made and the coach that was in charge made in Jesse Marsch, that I would say with chest that Weston McKennie is arguably a worse signing than JKA, and JKA played about 15 minutes.

That's how strongly I feel about that. He seemed disinterested. We saw nowhere near the player that he's shown in the past, as you've mentioned.

That is a big statement that might have been in the heat of the moment, but there was no doubt that McKennie was a big disappointment at Elland Road.

Leeds signed the USA international on loan in January 2023 until the end of the season, with the option to make the deal permanent for ££30m.

Speaking on his arrival, McKennie when asked what he was going to bring, said: "Hard work, non-stop 90 minutes back and forth, tackles, trash talk probably and everything - funny moments and serious moments.

It's a dream come true, I've dreamed of playing in the Premier League for the longest time. To have the support system I have here, I'm ready.

Some Leeds fans would certainly question the work-rate they saw from the American in the final months of the season, as they were relegated from the Premier League and McKennie returned to his parent club.

Having failed to reach the levels he had done earlier in his career during his time in Yorkshire, it would have been a surprise to supporters that his career has continued on an upward trajectory, with the 28-year-old establishing himself as a key player for Juve.

McKennie scored nine goals and registered eight assists for the Serie A giants last term, including netting four times in the Champions League, taking his career total in the competition to 11.

McKennie may not be a well-loved figure at Leeds after the role he played in their demise that season, but he has shown the quality he has in his locker, which makes his Elland Road spell even more disappointing.

Mortgage Applications Dip as Rates Hit Three-Week High

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

House keys with a house shaped keychain resting on a mortgage application form

Mortgage applications dipped slightly this week, in keeping with the average 30-year mortgage rate rising to a three-week high.

The latest Market Composite Index from the Mortgage Bankers Association (MBA)—its measure of mortgage loan activity volume and includes purchases and refinances—shows mortgage application activity decreasing 2.3% on a seasonally adjusted basis after last week’s 6.2% increase. On an unadjusted basis, the Index decreased 5% compared with the previous week.

This week’s decline follows weeks of fluctuations as the 30-year fixed mortgage rate still solidly remains over 7%–last week decreasing to 7.11% according to Freddie Mac but currently sitting at 7.46%, according to Mortgage News Daily. Freddie Mac’s next rate report will be released Thursday.

“Mortgage applications dipped as mortgage rates moved higher, with the 30-year fixed mortgage rate up to its highest level in three weeks at 7.35 percent,” said Joel Kan, CMB, MBA’s vice president and deputy chief economist. “Purchase applications were down 18 percent from a year ago, as renewed affordability challenges from elevated mortgage rates have pushed prospective homebuyers to the sidelines.”

MBA’s Refinance Index decreased 2% from the previous week and was 54% lower than the same week one year ago. MBA’s seasonally adjusted Purchase Index–its weekly measurement of nationwide home loan applications based on a sample of about 75% of U.S. mortgage activity–decreased 3% from one week earlier. The unadjusted Purchase Index decreased 7% compared with the previous week and was 18% lower than the same week one year ago, according to the report.

The refinance share of mortgage activity saw an increase this week–to 41.1% from 39.8% the previous week, with Kan pointing to a rise in FHA refinances as most notable.

“Overall refinance activity was down over the week, but FHA refinances increased almost 7 percent,” Kan stated. “The FHA rate was around 30 basis points below the conforming rate, which supported refinance opportunities for some homeowners.”

The FHA share of total applications increased to 16.1% from 15.5% the week prior, with MBA listing the VA share of total applications increasing to 12.8% from 12.4% the week prior. The USDA share of total applications remained unchanged at 0.5 percent from the week prior.

Kan also pointed to additional macroeconomic indicators as potentially weighing on home-purchase application activity into the new year.

“Treasury yields rose last week amid data showing inflation accelerated in October and Fed officials acknowledging that additional rate hikes may be needed in the coming months,” he said.

Nvidia interim CEO Kress tells employees ‘let’s move forward’ at first all-hands meeting since Jensen Huang exit

📅 2026-11-18  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Interim Nvidia CEO Colette Kress told employees "let's move forward" on Wednesday during the company's first all-hands meeting since founder Jensen Huang resigned, according to an attendee who spoke to CNBC on the condition of anonymity.

Kress was joined by newly appointed board chair Harvey Jones and hyperscale and high-performance computing head Ian Buck. Several other senior members of Nvidia's executive team spoke, including long-time company veteran Jeff Fisher, senior vice president of GPU engineering Jonah Alben, executive vice president in charge of Operations Debora Shoquist and co-founder Chris Malachowsky, according to a person familiar with the matter.

Kress opened with the message about moving forward, and also brought up pay raises for employees, according to the attendee. Jones then pivoted to a discussion about the search for the next CEO, the attendee added. The meeting lasted about an hour and was in person as well as streamed on Microsoft Teams.

The general sentiment swirling in the room, according to the attendee, is that employees want Huang to come back. Employees have spoken up on X in recent days to call for the return of Huang as CEO, and one senior employee reportedly sent a letter to the board warning Nvidia would lose "irreplaceable talent" if it doesn't consider reinstating Huang.

But another person familiar with the matter disagreed, saying that employees welcomed the remarks from leadership as well as the visibility into company leadership.

"The Nvidia board unanimously accepted his resignation and does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," Jones said during the earnings call on Tuesday.

Despite recent turmoil, Wednesday's meeting was not out of the ordinary. Nvidia typically holds all-hands the morning after quarterly earnings reports, according to a former employee who spoke to CNBC on the condition of anonymity. Kress recapped Tuesday's earnings report, calling it a "great quarter," according to one person familiar with the matter.

In past meetings, Huang took time to answer questions submitted by employees, according to the former employee.

On Tuesday, Huang said he would be "honored to answer these calls and return as CEO."

Nvidia shares were down 2% on Wednesday, a day after the company reported third-quarter earnings that topped Wall Street expectations and gave no signal that the company was going to slow its growth. Still, Nvidia faced several downgrades from Wall Street on Wednesday, including from Bernstein and Evercore.

Hims & Hers is launching five compounded peptide treatments not approved by the FDA

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Hims & Hers customers may be prescribed Sermorelin, NAD+, Glutathione, Vitamin B12 and L-Carnitine after a provider visit

Hims & Hers on Wednesday launched Peptides+, a specialty offering five compounded treatments that the Food and Drug Administration has not approved, according to CNBC.

The company said customers who see a licensed provider for a clinical appointment may receive a personalized plan that includes sermorelin, NAD+, glutathione, vitamin B12 and L-carnitine. The treatments are prepared domestically, and licensed U.S. pharmacies that answer to state pharmacy regulators dispense them, the company said. Hims & Hers said its Hims brand is offering the treatments to men and its Hers brand to women.

Hims & Hers plans to add BPC-157 and TB-500 "consistent with regulatory standards," the company said. Neither peptide is approved, and the bulk of the studies so far involved animal subjects instead of people, according to CNBC.

Peptides+ is available with the company's Care+ program. Care+ covers biannual lab work, unlimited help and regular check-ins with a team of dedicated clinicians, plus around-the-clock messaging with providers who have training in peptide treatment, the company said.

The launch follows an October policy from the Department of Health and Human Services. Under that policy, the FDA has said it will hold off on enforcement against licensed pharmacies, federal facilities and physicians who compound and dispense six peptides that lack approval, for as long as formal rulemaking is being reviewed, according to CNBC. The policy does not signal that regulators have cleared those individual peptides.

"Anyone investing in their health deserves to know exactly what they're putting in their body, and to have dedicated support along the way," Anant Vinjamoori, chief medical officer of Hims, said in a statement.

Vinjamoori also acknowledged gaps in the evidence. "We don't have all of this data that we're used to having with compounds like this," he told CNBC. He said patients with serious conditions such as cardiovascular, liver or kidney disease won't qualify for the compounds at this stage.

"There is a difference between this and FDA-approved therapies," Vinjamoori told Fierce Healthcare.

Outside experts have questioned the approach. "Without actual testing, all you have is anecdotal information, and that's just not enough information to put a substance in your body," Joshua Kritzer, a chemistry professor at Tufts University, told CNBC.

To No One’s Surprise, Trump’s Handpicked Navy Secretary Just as Incompetent and Heartless as He Is

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hung Cao’s confirmation hearing went just about as badly as any I’ve ever seen.

Maybe, like me, you had a bad day on Tuesday. But, I assure you, nobody on this planet had a worse day on Tuesday than did Hung Cao, the acting Secretary of the Navy who met the Senate Armed Services Committee on Tuesday in pursuit of being confirmed in that position. He left the hearing, as the old boxing writers used to put it, in a blanket.

New Newcastle United stadium go ahead imminent after financiers met club hierarchy

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

It appears we may not have long to wait now for the much anticipated new Newcastle United stadium decision to be announced.

A media exclusive on Wednesday giving a headline making update.

Craig Hope at The Mail revealing that talks over a period of time between the Newcastle United hierarchy and Goldman Sachs reached a key point this month.

With senior representatives from the American investment banking organisation travelling to Tyneside this month.

The Mail reporting that their informed sources have told them that this significant meeting was to finalise ‘the proposed funding structure for a spectacular new 65,000-seater home on Leazes Park’ for Newcastle United, rather than an attempt to rebuild/restructure parts of St James’ Park.

The report talks of a brand new Newcastle United stadium costing £1.3billion super stadium – the strongest indication yet that the club are preparing to leave St James’ Park.

I have never thought that a major redevelopment of the existing stadium was viable, the cost would be huge compared to how few extra seats it would allow and just as importantly, the limitations on upgrading the existing ‘normal’ seating areas and the hospitality ones to the level of modern new stadiums that have been built in recent times. Not to mention the fact that if say the Gallowgate end was redeveloped, it would lead to a hugely reduced capacity for a significant amount of time.

The man from The Mail has also revealed that the meeting coincided with Jacobo Solis having arrived on Tyneside. The report detailing that ‘the influential Public Investment Fund executive and club director who is rarely seen in the city, was also present. The Spaniard’s involvement is viewed as extremely significant by those close to the project, and we understand an announcement on the club’s stadium plans is now ‘very close’.’

Back in September 2025 The Times revealed: ‘There is now a core of PIF executives who have been involved with Newcastle United since day one of its ownership, including Jacobo Solis, who, along with being head of European direct investments at PIF, is now also on the Newcastle board, having joined at the start of 2025.’

The Mail report that: ‘The proposed funding model combines borrowing, owner investment and potentially outside investors, with the debt sitting with Newcastle rather than the ownership. That makes the involvement of Goldman Sachs particularly significant as the club look to turn their ambitious plans into a financially viable reality.’

The media exclusive concludes with an expectation that an official announcement on the new Newcastle United stadium will be made early in 2027.

Arm's CEO is being courted as a candidate to replace Jensen Huang at Nvidia

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Recruiter Heidrick & Struggles has begun reaching out to possible candidates after Jensen Huang left Nvidia last week

Arm Holdings CEO Rene Haas has been contacted by Heidrick & Struggles, the search firm Nvidia $NVDA's board retained to find Jensen Huang's replacement, and asked about taking the top job at the chipmaker, according to Bloomberg.

Heidrick has begun sounding out potential candidates for the position in the days since Huang's departure last week, according to Bloomberg. Nvidia representatives could not be reached for comment.

Nvidia Board Chair Harvey Jones said on the company's earnings call on Tuesday that the board has brought on Heidrick & Struggles to run the search for its next CEO. Jones said directors will consider both insiders and outsiders and will carry out a "thorough search." He also said the board "does not intend to reinstate" Huang.

Jeff Sanders, co-managing partner of Heidrick’s global CEO and board directors practice, is leading the search, according to The Wall Street Journal. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO while keeping her CFO role.

Huang resigned last week after admitting to an affair with a former executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer. On Tuesday, in his first public remarks since stepping down, he said he would be open to returning if the directors asked.

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent pushed the board over the weekend to hire someone from outside the company. “They need a dramatic shakeup,” Bessent said on Fox News.

Disney’s Experiences Segment Hit Record Quarterly Revenue of $10.1 Billion as Cruise Days Surged. Can the Momentum Last?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many consumers long to ride on a large ship these days.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 18, 2026 at 2:00PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Walt Disney ›

It isn't easy for a huge, sprawling global company to increase its key fundamentals at double-digit percentage rates. Walt Disney (DIS +4.74%) achieved this feat with the fourth-quarter and annual fiscal 2026 results it published Tuesday morning.

All three of the entertainment giant's business units posted revenue gains in the mid-teens; arguably the most impressive of the trio was experiences, which notched an all-time quarterly record by drawing more than $10.1 billion in revenue. Let's take a brief overview of how Disney did during the quarter, and drill down into the performance of the experiences unit.

For the period, Disney's total revenue was just under $25.95 billion, a 16% improvement over the fourth quarter of 2025's tally -- although I should note that the most recent quarter included one more week. Regardless, that percentage essentially matched the growth of all three business units (entertainment and sports, in addition to experiences).

With such lift, non-GAAP (adjusted) net income also soared. It headed skyward at a 56% clip to almost $3.35 billion, or $1.77 per share.

Both numbers beat the consensus analyst estimates, albeit not spectacularly. The average prognosticator forecasts for revenue and per-share profitability were $25.21 billion and $1.68, respectively.

Experiences is a close second in terms of revenue among the three reporting units, behind entertainment. Yet it was by far the No. 1 when measured by operating income, with its $2.25 billion trouncing entertainment's $1.33 billion and sports' $1.39 billion. It also happened to be the champ in the year-ago quarter too.

I should note here that, starting in the current (first) quarter, Disney has shifted its consumer products licensing and publishing operations from experiences to entertainment. If the company had made that move before the fourth quarter, experiences would still be the runner-up in revenue. Meanwhile, the adjustment would shift consumer products' operating profit to entertainment, and make entertainment No. 1 in that metric.

Nevertheless, experiences was a crucial driver of growth for those impressive top- and bottom-line improvements. Given that, it's worthwhile to explore why, and devote a little space to whether Disney is positioning it well for further growth.

As its name implies, experiences comprises the unique, in-person Disney attractions. This includes the theme parks, anchored by the endlessly popular Disneyland in Southern California, and comprises the burgeoning Disney Cruise business and travel services (plus the consumer products operation that's now under entertainment's big tent).

What gives the unit its notably higher margins is that many of its operations center around fixed infrastructure. Disneyland and other parks are already built, and the company's cruise ships regularly travel the seas. Yes, there are maintenance costs, but for the most part much of the capex for those assets has already been spent, while the customers keep coming (and paying). In the sports division, content costs are extremely high and seem to be on the rise, so it's more challenging to make a buck. As for entertainment, we can say the same for even a mid-budget movie or a network TV show.

Happily for the company's most profitable unit, we're famously in the midst of an experiences boom, as consumers with money to spend are eager to participate in unique events. This explains the recent, sustained success of the travel industry; have you noticed how often commercial airplanes fly at near or full capacity these days?

One experience consumers long to enjoy is a cruise. Disney was happy to oblige, as its cruise days (total passengers carried times the total duration of sailings) popped by 11%. Management had smartly laid the groundwork for this rise by adding two new ships -- the Destiny and the Adventure -- to its fleet between November 2025 and this past March. The total is now eight ships, and there's clearly enough demand to keep those sailings lively.

The experience boom was gathering steam as the COVID pandemic faded in 2023. That year, Disney announced it planned to almost double its capital expenditures (capex) in the experiences segment, to roughly $60 billion over the decade from fiscal years 2024 to 2033. And part of this was the expansion of the fleet, hence the recent debuts of Destiny and Adventure.

That's still very much a central strategy for Disney. Of the capex splurge on experiences, CEO Josh D'Amaro said on the fourth-quarter conference call, "the plan will continue to expand our capacity and deliver a differentiated entertainment experience as we continue turbocharging our parks and cruise businesses."

All this, of course, leads to the question of whether Disney stock is a buy. After all, judging by Mr. Market's reaction to the earnings -- the shares closed Tuesday down by 1.5% -- a clutch of investors didn't think so. They flagged some negatives, such as the 7% slide in operating income in the international theme park segment and the anticipated 9% annual rise in spending on content in the new fiscal year.

However, as a longtime Disney shareholder and bull (with some down moments over the years, admittedly), I think the fourth quarter's stars well overshadowed the slumps, to the point where the company had scope for its very generous 33% dividend raise. Plus, its focus on experiences is already powering strong growth, and will surely continue to do so. This is one stock I'm absolutely keeping in my portfolio.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 794%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 203% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

How NBC’s Longtime Page Program Has Served as a Key Stepping Stone to Media Leadership

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Katie Hockmeyer now oversees “Saturday Night Live” as NBC’s executive vice president of late night. But she still can’t forget her first brush with the sketch comedy staple: As an NBC page assigned to the floor.

That’s right, Hockmeyer was a real-life Kenneth the Page, just like the one played by Jack McBrayer at the “TGS” production offices on “30 Rock.” In 2002, Hockmeyer was the NBC desk page at “SNL,” right when it was populated by some of the show’s all-time greats — including Tina Fey (who eventually created “30 Rock”), Maya Rudolph, Jimmy Fallon, Amy Poehler, Fred Armisen and Will Forte. Related Stories Amazon Debuts $30 Streaming Bundle That Includes Starz, BritBox, AMC+, MGM+ and PBS Masterpiece In One Place Alycia Baumgardner vs. Elif Nur Turhan on ESPN Unlimited: Here’s Where to Watch the Title Bout Live Online

“It was incredible,” she says. One of her duties was to escort that week’s guest host around 30 Rock. “I remember walking Mick Jagger from down in the basement up to Lorne Michaels’ office as a page. The idea that someone would say, ‘I designate you to walk Mick Jagger’ is so crazy to me! But they trust you to learn to be professional out of the gate. Honestly, it was an amazing experience, and I learned very quickly that I like being in a live environment with comedians.”

Hockmeyer clearly impressed the “SNL” team: After her stint as a page was over, she was asked to interview as one of Michaels’ assistants. She got the job — and eventually worked her way up to running the entire late night department at NBC.

“I only hire pages,” she says. “It’s the best post-collegiate program. You talk about the history of company. You basically meet everyone in the building. The access that you have is pretty incredible.”

Hockmeyer is one of many success stories to come out of the NBC page program. Other notable alum include former Disney CEO Michael Eisner, news broadcaster Ted Koppel, “Beef ” creator Lee Sung Jin, “The View” co-host Sara Haines, late TV host Regis Philbin, “Charlie’s Angels” star Kate Jackson and actress Aubrey Plaza (who even wore her old NBC page uniform on stage while hosting “SNL” in 2023).

NBC’s page program dates back to 1933, during the golden age of radio, and began as a way to assist in guest relations, ushering, tour services and more at the thenbrand new Rockefeller Plaza.

But over the past decade, the NBC page gig has become much more of a structured career-learning experience that acts like a paid apprenticeship with a beginning, middle and end. The highly competitive program selects around 22 to 25 people in New York and 12 to 15 in Los Angeles three times a year for what’s become a TV business and NBC boot camp.

According to Priany Hadiatmodjo, senior director of the NBC page program, around 3,000 people apply for each cycle to get one of those coveted spots.

“It’s a pretty rigorous process to get in,” she says. “We read every single application. There’s a video interview component that comes next, where you have to answer some questions that help us understand who you are. And then the final stage of the application process is a panel interview. The process is not for the faint of heart. A lot of people apply multiple times. Sometimes they get in on their second or even third try. I don’t think there’ll ever be a shortage of people who are really eager to work in this industry. But once you’re here, the expectation to excel is probably harder than the actual process itself.”

After an immersive two-week onboarding where they meet with and learn from NBCU leadership, pages are assigned to different portions of the business for 18 weeks at a time. The divisions where pages can work has been greatly expanded over the past 10 years to all facets of NBCU, and pages can express an interest in which divisions they’d like to work.

“I always like to say that if my team and I are doing our jobs right, the page program serves as a microcosm of NBCU,” says Hadiatmodjo. “Some people have called it the most intense masters in media that you could get. We’re more intentional about the assignments we offer, and those assignments are typically within parts of the company that are very future-focused. Because the hope is that we’re finding the best and brightest out there, and that they’ll one day become leaders of this industry. So we’re trying to put them into spaces, places and environments that will really challenge them daily and give them the experience of what it’s really like to work at NBCU.”

Once an NBC page has completed a year in the program and worked in three different departments, they can start to apply for jobs inside the company.

“By then they’ve hopefully been on a path of self-discovery and figured out some things that they really love doing,” Hadiatmodjo adds. “It really is a year of self-discovery and experimentation, and really understanding what this industry is all about. Our responsibility is making sure they’re ready to hit the ground running in their next early career role. Our ultimate goal is to find that talent, cultivate it, and hopefully, see them stick around.”

And that’s come true throughout the company, with execs like Hockmeyer. More than 20 years after she was a page, the experience is still top of mind. “I could give you a tour of 30 Rock right now,” she says. “I can tell you about the floating studio floor in 8H, where Arturo Toscanini used to perform. I can still remember a couple of my key talking points!”

29 states will require finance course in high school by 2031, report predicts

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Ten states received an “A” for requiring standalone personal finance courses in the National Endowment for Financial Education’s latest report.

“We’re excited about the progress this year’s report shows,” said Taylor Stanek, managing director of policy and advocacy for NEFE. “There is momentum and movement behind the effort to create a national expectation that students receive financial education in high school.”

The center's research involved reviews of each states' approach to personal finance education in high schools, state academic standards on the topic, and related laws, guidelines and regulations.

Broadly speaking, states with an “A” grade have shown they are implementing financial education with fidelity, while those receiving a “B” or “C” offer some level of financial education but short of a full semester course. These states also may have some degree of requirements in statewide academic standards.

States receiving a “D” or “F” have little or nothing to offer at the moment, Stanek said, expressing optimism that all 50 states will receive an “A” eventually.

“There are a few things we are keeping a close eye on as states move forward,” he said. “Implementation is key; passing one of these requirements is just a start."

The states also need to make sure schools are offering the courses as expected, as a standalone commitment, with teachers who have been trained, and have curricular materials available to meet the needs of specific students, Stanek said.

"This is an opportunity for every state … to make sure they’re evaluating the effectiveness of these programs and maximizing their benefit and impact,” Stanek said. Each state takes a somewhat different approach based on their unique range of needs, he added.

Utah implemented the first semester-long financial education requirement in 2008 and conducts statewide audits on programs, while Missouri was the second state in 2010, Stanek said. Even states with “C” grades or lower have seen progress in certain localities, with Denver Public Schools adopting this ahead of the state of Colorado, for example, he said.

“In the majority of other states, there’s at least something in place,” he added. “It’s ensuring that it’s offered across the board — requiring it to make sure every student receives it. When it’s an elective, students of color, low-income students and female students are less likely to take it.”

The figures from the 2025 national opinion poll underscore why the momentum has grown, especially at a time of economic uncertainty, Stanek said. “There’s a recognition that people need extra support to be [financially] successful,” he said.

“This is no longer a niche side topic. Folks are saying this is something worthy of being a core academic discipline in our K-12 system.”

The previous five editions of the report were published by the Center for Financial Literacy at Champlain College in Burlington, Vt.

49ers’ Kyle Shanahan faces his 0-5 kryptonite in Mexico City

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

History is not on Kyle Shanahan's side when the 49ers take on the Vikings in Mexico City Sunday.

Minnesota's defensive coordinator Brian Flores has never lost when facing Shanahan as a head coach or offensive assistant. Shanahan is 0-5 against Flores.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan looks on during the second quarter against the Seattle Seahawks at Lumen Field in Seattle, Oct. 11, 2026. Getty Images

The most memorable Shanahan-Flores matchup was in Super Bowl LI, where the 49ers head coach was Falcons offensive coordinator and Flores was linebackers coach for the Patriots. The Falcons famously blew a 28-3 lead with New England taking the game in overtime.

As things stand, Flores leading a defense with Shanahan leading an offense has never resulted in a win for the latter.

Brian Flores, defensive coordinator, of the Minnesota Vikings celebrates after the game against the Chicago Bears at U.S. Bank Stadium on Dec. 16, 2024, in Minneapolis, Minnesota. Getty Images

But the 49ers have seemingly rewritten history all season long, starting with their season opener against the Rams in Australia. Los Angeles was dubbed as the Super Bowl favorite with an undefeated season being thrown out as a possibility for the would-be LA juggernaut.

San Francisco quickly ruined that narrative by handing the Rams an absolute walloping in Week 1.

Brock Purdy (13) of the San Francisco 49ers looks to pass against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas. Getty Images

Quarterback Brock Purdy has also defied history with an MVP-worthy performance in 2026. As the last pick in the 2022 NFL Draft, Purdy was never supposed to be a star, let alone a franchise quarterback.

Injuries have plagued the 49ers this season, as they have in recent years, often derailing their Super Bowl hopes.

But Shanahan has still guided the offense to the top of the NFL in several statistical categories while keeping the team at the top of the standings.

Overall, the 49ers can afford to lose a game with an 8-1 record. But it would be hasty to immediately rule the squad out of a win in Mexico City.

Basque Cinema Expands Its (Rapidly) Growing Footprint in Tallinn

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With Basque filmmakers in the Tallinn Spotlight section and buzzy competition title 'Caballé,' the never-healthier regional production hub is reaching territories not yet conquered: "It opens different doors."

This year, Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival (PÖFF) is letting Basque cinema bask — pun intended — in the spotlight.

Through dedicated investment over the last 10 years, the Basque film and TV output has never been stronger. The region has attracted productions from all over the world thanks to an alluring 60 percent tax rebate — increasing to 70 percent for projects shot in the Basque language, Euskara — allowing the area to lay claim to Catherine Zeta-Jones’ Kill Jackie and Nanni Moretti’s It Will Happen Tonight, among a slew of Spanish projects that catapulted the region to a record 45 Goya nominations earlier this year. Related Stories TV 'Slow Horses' Actress Ruth Bradley to Be Honored at the Oscar Wilde Awards (Exclusive) Movies 'In Starland': How Ray Panthaki, in His Directing Debut, Cast Clarence Maclin in His First Lead Role and Made a Very Personal Film Built on Meritocracy

This week, Tallinn’s Spotlight section represents the booming Basque audiovisual in territory not yet conquered. “It opens different doors,” begins Laura Lizuma, a Latvian producer based in San Sebastian who comes to the festival as part of the Basque delegation this year. “If the Basque country was in the spotlight in Argentina and Spain, that would be understandable — but the Basque industry is very small and when we think about global cinema… It’s a great place to discover new possibilities of co-production because [when else] would a Basque producer come to Estonia or the Baltic states?”

Lizuma arrives in the Estonian capital as a producer on The River Woman, one of three Basque projects selected as part of the Tallinn Spotlight to receive development support. The feature film, directed by Tamara García Iglesias and co-written with Jon Bilbao, is described by Lizuma as “a rather unconventional western — one that takes the familiar codes of the genre and turns them around to tell a story from a woman’s perspective.” García’s film is one of many Basque films hitting this Tallinn edition, joined by the likes of Daniel Romero Bueno’s debut feature The Daughters (Las Hijas) in the Midnight Shivers section and Joana Moya Blanco’s La Koreana from the festival’s industry program.

But the buzziest title of all competes in Tallinn’s official competition selection. Caballé, from Venezuelan filmmaker Patricia Ortega, is a biographical portrait of Spanish opera diva Montserrat Caballé that shot mostly in Bilbao. The movie is a Spain-led co-production between Set Màgic Audiovisual, La Claqueta, Basque company Amania Films, La Charito and Belgium’s Menuetto Film, with Film Factory Entertainment handling international sales.

“I was interested in her contradictions,” Ortega begins to The Hollywood Reporter about what drew her to the famed opera singer, depicted in the film by Begoña Alberdi. “Her strength and vulnerability, her doubts and insecurities, but also her determination to make her way in a world dominated by men.”

Ortega describes the Basque contribution as “an essential part of bringing Caballé to the screen.” This doesn’t just refer to the production side of things, but also to the talent, commitment and “generosity” of those who worked on the film. “Montserrat Caballé was not only a legend, she was also a deeply multicultural woman,” explained the director. “She spoke several languages and worked with people from all over the world. In a way, that is something we experienced while making the film in the Basque Country: people from different places and backgrounds coming together around a shared creative vision. I think she would have appreciated that spirit of collaboration.”

She vouches for the work of the Basque crews and support from the region’s government, saying that Bilbao’s logistical strengths made the filming process “much easier.” Ortega added: “There was also the experience of living in the city for several weeks. We found a place that welcomed us with open arms, with its remarkable variety of landscapes, the generosity and warmth of its people, and its rich and diverse cultural life.”

Those virtues are on display and then some in Tallinn, where international producers are able to get a sense of the strength of the Basque audiovisual industry and production capabilities. That’s according to Esther Cabero Gonzalo, the shorts programmer at Zineuskadi, a company that works strategically to promote the Basque audiovisual sector, partnering with every production output in the region and working with the local government to help facilitate co-productions, distribution and sales deals, as well as securing films a coveted slot at major film festivals.

“Having a delegation in Tallinn creates opportunities to meet people, establish direct contacts and develop potential collaborations,” says Cabero, who adds that a representative for the Etxepare Basque Institute, the organization leading the promotion of Basque culture internationally, will be talking in Tallinn about the tempting tax incentives. “I think all these elements help demonstrate just how much can be achieved in such a small territory,” says Cabero.

The Basque shorts program, Kimuak, had one film — Raúl Barreras’s Meanwhile, Then — premiere in the PÖFF Shorts section over the weekend. Barrera’s feature follows five researchers from the future who arrive in a valley in the Basque Country to record gestures and emotions that have disappeared over time. Through interviews and observations of daily life, they delve into the private lives of the inhabitants — but soon the framework underpinning their research begins to crumble.

Six shorts from previous editions are set to screen through the rest of the festival as part of the Basque Spotlight. Cabero is full of pride showcasing such a broad range of work from male and female filmmakers, as well as documentaries, animations and more, to the crowds in Tallinn. “We can promote our culture, our language, our identity, and make connections with international audiences,” she says. “But it’s also a way to promote those names, the directors, so they can find opportunities abroad and co-productions.”

For Cabero, it isn’t just the draw of Basque talent, production companies, studios and technical facilities that has helped make the Spanish region a thriving film and TV hub, but the historic towns, industrial environments, as well as forests and dramatic natural scenery.

Interestingly enough, it’s also Lizuma’s appreciation for the Basque Country’s sweeping landscapes that will help The River Woman achieve high-scale cinema at a lower cost. “With this project, in particular, the first idea is to shoot in the Basque Country because of the nature,” says Lizuma. “We are planning to shoot between the Basque Country and Navarre, but this project is developed from the beginning to be an international co-production. So we are looking for other territories that we could film [in], […] and we really have the intention to get those woods or those rivers here in [the] Basque Country or the Baltic states,” she says. Basque cinema, she adds, often capitalizes on its “pretty strong” relationship with nature.

Lizuma has lived in Spain for 10 years now and can measure in great depth the success of the concentrated backing of the Basque film and TV sector. “There are a lot of projects coming [to the] Basque Country […] because they can get this tax incentive. So there are a lot of projects that come there with this interest, but also for Basque directors and producers, there are a lot more projects that are not so small anymore. When I came, there were a lot of self-discoveries — very small, very local. Now there are projects that raises three million [euros] in funding. That was impossible before.”

And while Ortega’s award hopeful filmed mostly in one of the region’s busiest and most vibrant cities, what strikes her about Basque cinema is its ability to simultaneously straddle a particular place and culture while speaking to audiences far beyond it. “There isn’t just one Basque cinematic voice,” she says, “there are many filmmakers with distinct sensibilities, and that diversity is one of its great strengths.”

She touts the work of David Pérez Sañudo, who recently presented the brilliant Sacamantecas, Alauda Ruiz de Azúa with the five-time Goya Award-winning Los domingos, and Estibaliz Urresola’s 20,000 Species of Bees, shortlisted for best international feature at the 2024 Oscars. “They are examples of the quality of the work coming out of the Basque Country,” says Ortega.

“I would also like to mention Nerea Torrijos, who was the head of costume design on Caballé. She is a wonderful example of how Basque talent extends across every area of filmmaking,” Ortega adds. This particularly personal testament is indicative of the kind of relationships the Basque audiovisual industry has so far managed to cultivate. Will Tallinn also succumb to the region’s undeniable charm?

Newsmax sued to block Fox from integrating its $22 billion Roku deal

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Newsmax says the combined Fox-Roku would hold more than 70% of free ad-supported streaming TV, according to its complaint

Newsmax sued Fox in California federal court on Wednesday to stop the company from integrating Roku $ROKU into its operations, arguing the $22 billion merger harms competition, according to Bloomberg.

According to Bloomberg, the complaint says combining Roku with Tubi, Fox's free streaming service, would hand Fox over 70% of the free ad-supported streaming TV market. Newsmax argues that Fox, owner of the Fox News channel, has a motive to put Newsmax at a disadvantage in streaming television.

Fox completed its acquisition of Roku on Nov. 6. The Justice Department closed its antitrust investigation the same day and said Fox had committed to keeping Roku an open and neutral content partner.

The commitments include keeping non-Fox content available on Roku for a minimum of three years and refraining from discriminating against others' content in search results, according to Bloomberg. The Justice Department did not file the commitments in federal court, which skipped a public review by a judge.

Newsmax calls the commitments inadequate and unenforceable by a court, according to Bloomberg. Newsmax says it requested that Fox sign a binding contract, but Fox refused. Fox did not respond to a request for comment.

Fox announced the deal in June. In September, the company said it expected the deal to close in the first half of 2027, after the Justice Department sent second requests for information to Fox and Roku.

Under the terms, Roku stockholders receive $95.30 in cash and 0.9799 shares of Fox Class A common stock for each Roku share. Fox said it expects about $400 million of run-rate cost synergies within two years of closing.

"Beginning today, our top priority for the combined company is to continue to relentlessly serve audiences and partners, while reinforcing the openness and choice that is the essence of the Roku platform," Fox Executive Chair and CEO Lachlan Murdoch said in a statement at closing.

Trump admin orders Kalshi to halt wagers on what words Trump will say

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

CFTC: Kalshi bets may violate US ban on contracts that can be easily manipulated.

The Trump administration ordered prediction market Kalshi to stop offering event contracts that pay out based on whether President Trump will say a specific word before a particular date. Kalshi halted trading in the contracts while the US Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) conducts a review into whether the Trump mention contracts violate federal law.

CNBC reported yesterday that “Kalshi quietly paused trading in a handful of ‘mention’ contracts tied to President Donald Trump at the end of last week at the request of the CFTC.” Kalshi told media outlets that it is complying with the CFTC order.

The markets taken down by Kalshi allowed users to wager on whether Trump would say certain words, including nicknames and the names of countries and companies. A similar expired event contract that can still be viewed on Kalshi’s website asked whether Trump would use the nicknames “Marjorie Traitor Greene,” “Sleepy Joe,” and “Whack Job” or “Wack Job” before April 2026.

The CFTC order issued to Kalshi last week applies broadly to “the TRUMPSAY contract series.” The event contracts in this series include trades in the format, “Will [word] be said by Donald Trump after Issuance and before [date]?” The CFTC order also applied to a specific event contract that asks, “What nicknames will Trump say before 2027?”

The CFTC order came several months after the federal agency settled charges against a Trump teleprompter aide who made over $100,000 in Kalshi wagers on what Trump would say in prepared remarks. On August 28, the CFTC required the man, Gabriel Perez, to disgorge profits of $107,539.02 and pay a civil penalty of $65,000.

“In his position, Perez had access to presidential speeches prior to those speeches being delivered and Perez misappropriated that information—in breach of his duty of trust and confidence—to trade presidential mention market contracts, generating over $107,500 in profits,” the CFTC said at the time.

Senate Democrats recently said the White House hasn’t taken meaningful action against insider trading, “all while the president and his family stand to personally profit from their participation in prediction markets.” Prediction markets have flourished under the Trump administration’s permissive regulatory approach. The CFTC has allowed prediction markets to offer sports gambling nationwide and filed lawsuits against states that attempt to regulate Kalshi under their gambling laws.

Kalshi recently dropped Donald Trump Jr. as an advisor, saying it made the decision because Trump Jr.’s venture capital firm increased its investment in its competitor, Polymarket. Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour also acknowledged that the “optics” of Trump Jr.’s advisory role weren’t good for Kalshi. Trump Jr. still stands to benefit from Kalshi’s success, because he was given equity shares in the company.

The CFTC has said that mention markets are easily manipulated but reviews them on a case-by-case basis instead of prohibiting them. Prediction markets can “self-certify” that any new event contract complies with US law and start offering it without waiting for the CFTC’s permission, but the CFTC can halt trading afterward based on reviews of specific contracts.

The Commodity Exchange Act (CEA) prohibits the listing of contracts that are readily susceptible to manipulation. CFTC staff issued a September advisory about the legality of markets on what people will say, and whether they will attend an event or interact with another person. The advisory said these contracts may be viewed as “presumptively readily susceptible to manipulation,” because they “may be controlled by a single individual, a small group of individuals, or persons with access to or influence over the individual whose words, attendance, or interaction determines settlement.”

The CFTC said this type of contract requires measures “to prevent manipulation and misappropriation of material non-public information,” and that Kalshi failed to comply with the CFTC staff advisory. About “a week after the Staff Advisory was released, Kalshi listed the Contract [on nicknames] for trading without amending the [TRUMPSAY] Contract Series’ self-certification (or submitting a new self-certification) to make the ‘heightened showing’ of non-manipulability required by the Staff Advisory,” the order said.

The CFTC said it will conduct “a proceeding to determine whether the certification of the Contracts was false—that is, whether the Contracts comply with the Act, including the Core Principles, and Commission regulations thereunder as Kalshi has certified.” So far, “Kalshi has not adequately demonstrated that the Contracts are not readily susceptible to manipulation” as required by law, the order said.

“Given the available supporting evidence, including that Kalshi has not attempted to establish that the Contracts are non-manipulable with respect to the Staff Advisory’s guidance, the Commission presumptively believes the certification is false,” the order said.

The CFTC order is not a determination on whether the Trump mention markets can meet the standard of not being readily susceptible to manipulation. Rather, it is a determination that Kalshi has so far failed to show that the contracts are not easily manipulated.

The order “resolves only that, based on the current record, Kalshi has not demonstrated that the Contracts as certified comply with the Act and that the Contracts should not trade while the Commission completes its review,” the CFTC said.

We contacted Kalshi and will update this article if it provides a response.

Mansour defended the Trump mention contracts at an Axios event on Tuesday, according to Bloomberg. “What the president does say moves markets,” Mansour said. “So predicting what he’s about to say is actually pretty meaningful.”

The Bloomberg article noted that “Kalshi’s main rival Polymarket still has live mention markets tied to Trump’s speeches on its international website, which is not regulated by the CFTC.” Polymarket offers a separate US-regulated version of its prediction market.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

West Ham Exclusive: London Stadium Dispute Finally Resolved

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Claret & Hugh understands that West Ham and the London Stadium have reached an agreement to host a West Ham Women’s WSL game at the London Stadium on a date yet to be scheduled.

Late last year, the two parties had a dispute over a West Ham WSL game being played at the London Stadium and looked set for another legal battle in the courts.

Under the 2013 concessionaire agreement, West Ham are allowed to play up to 25 competitive games at the London Stadium in return for an annual usage fee.

The index-linked usage fee has inflated from £2.5m in 2016 to £4.8m each year in 2025, meaning each of the 25 games cost West Ham around £192,000 per match last season.

West Ham wanted to use one of those 25 competitive games to play a West Ham Women’s WSL game, which is mandated by regulations, but the London Stadium owners refused to allow it at the time.

The breakthrough came during negotiations for West Ham’s backing for the 2029 World Athletics Championships, which London Stadium bid to host.

The bid required West Ham’s backing, which was secured in August this year, but ultimately the bid failed in September, having lost out to Nairobi.

Part of the concessions agreed in return for West Ham’s backing for the athletics championship bid included hosting a WSL game at the London Stadium.

MTG Star Trek Card Featuring Patrick Stewart Sells At $26K

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Magic: The Gathering isn't exactly a stranger when it comes to ridiculously expensive cards, and while resellers aren't quite as rabid as some other TCGs, even somewhat less popular sets like Star Trek are still reaching hefty prices on the second-hand market. While the price might not compete with Lord of the Rings' over $2M One Ring card, getting one of MTG's new serialized cards will still cost you quite a bit.

While the seven hand-signed card variants introduced in MTG's Star Trek Collector's Boosters were already going to be a massive hit due to their limited availability, one of them has already broken the $20,000 mark, albeit for a bit of a morbid reason.

As shared by moombahh on Reddit, while most autographed Star Trek cards go for around $5-10K USD, a recent eBay listing for none other than Captain Jean-Luc Picard reportedly sold for a whopping $26K. While it's entirely possible the buyer sent a lower offer, it might even end up on the cheaper side of the spectrum if the other eBay listings are anything to go by.

With more recent listings having shot up all the way to $38K and an even more massive $45K, the price could wind up being well above the last sold price, and it's not hard to see why either.

For the first time ever, you can pack signed MTG cards, with seven to collect in total, but the first two revealed are of Captains Kirk and Janeway.

Dwarfing the Secret Lair card prices, the main reason players have been pricing their cards so high is that, since signing the cards, Jean-Luc Picard's actor, Patrick Stewart, recently passed away, leaving the serialized cards among the last items signed by the legendary actor.

While fans have pointed out that it's more than a little depressing that others are trying to capitalize on the late actor's passing by selling the cards for absurd prices, it's unlikely that sellers will be slowing down anytime soon, considering how limited their availability will continue to be.

Luka Doncic leadership on display as Lakers unexpected starter went bonkers

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Luka Dončić recognized what was happening.

He has been at the center of so many scoring flurries. He can tell when someone is about to have a hot hand. When an explosion is about to happen.

So when he saw the telltale signs coming from one of his teammates, he jumped into action, waving off Laker coach JJ Redick's play calls in the fourth quarter.

"No, I'm trying to get Q the ball," Doncic told Redick. "I'm trying to get Q."

Luka Doncic of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts after scoring against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. Getty Images

Quentin Grimes went on to have the most spectacular performance of his career, scoring a career-high 47 points in the Lakers' 118-114 overtime win over the Hornets.

"I got going and caught fire a little bit," Grimes said.

The 26-year-old guard had 21 points in the fourth quarter, singlehandedly outscoring the Hornets (17 points) in that period to help the Lakers claw their way back from an 18-point deficit.

On a night when the Lakers were without Austin Reaves and Walker Kessler, Grimes took over. On a night when Doncic was in foul trouble, Grimes played the hero.

It was obvious that Grimes was having a different kind of night at the top of the fourth quarter.

In a 23-second stretch, Grimes made a 3-pointer, stole the ball from Ryan Kalkbrenner and then drained another shot from beyond the arc to cut the Lakers' 12-point deficit to 6 points with 9:04 left.

Los Angeles Lakers guard Quentin Grimes reacts after making a 3-point shot during a game against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, in Los Angeles. Getty Images

From that moment forward, it was clear the ball was flying at a higher arc than usual when it left Grimes' fingertips. The rim had seemingly opened a little wider. He was having one of those nights where the ball seemed magnetized to the bottom of the net once it left his hands.

He went on to score 15 points in a row for the Lakers.

So, when the Lakers found themselves down 111-108 with 12.1 seconds left, there was no doubt who was going to take the final shot.

It wasn't the Slovenian superstar who's leading the league in scoring with 39 points per game.

It was the journeyman who had been traded three times since the LA Clippers selected him with the 25th pick in the 2021 draft, playing for the New York, Detroit, Dallas, Philadelphia and the Lakers over six seasons.

Quentin Grimes (5) of the Los Angeles Lakers dribbles during a game against the Charlotte Hornets in Los Angeles on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

So, after Matisse Thybulle chased down a missed free throw by Coby White, he passed the ball to Doncic, who dribbled down the court and stopped at the top of the 3-point line. Doncic pump-faked, then passed it to Grimes.

Grimes swished a 26-foot 3-pointer with 1.7 seconds left, sending the game into overtime with the score knotted at 111-111.

The shorthanded Lakers went on to pull off their ninth straight win, improving to 13-2, the best record in the league.

"What an amazing game from him, so it was unbelievable to watch," said Doncic, who had 36 points, six rebounds, seven assists and five fouls.

Quentin Grimes #5 of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a three-point basket during a game against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, in Los Angeles. NBAE via Getty Images

Grimes shot a stunning 10-for-14 from beyond the arc, becoming the only Laker to score 10 or more 3-pointers in a game other than Kobe Bryant.

"Man, I'm going to sleep good knowing that one tonight for sure," Grimes said. "But that's just crazy. Kobe's one of my idols growing up watching him. You try to model your game after him and his intensity, his passion for the game, that's just something that you don't see often.

"He's one of the reasons that I wanted to play this game. My dad was a Lakers fan from here and everything. So that's just crazy to have my name in a conversation with a legend like that."

After missing two games because of a sprained right pinky, Grimes returned Sunday against the Suns and went on to bank a game-sealing 3-pointer with 32.2 seconds left to lift the Lakers to a 129-121 win.

When asked if he called bank, he joked that he called game.

Then against the Hornets, Grimes had his magnum opus, surpassing his 46-point performance with the 76ers in March 2025.

"The hot-hand theory, I believe was prior debunked," Redick said. "Now it's been confirmed that it's a real thing. It definitely was a real thing tonight."

For Grimes, it was the type of game that can imbue him with confidence for an entire season.

It was the type of game that can tilt his trajectory.

"I'm calling this a statement game," he said.

Winterize Your Car Before the First Freeze – Start With a Free Battery Test

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Before the first cold snap, have your car’s battery tested free at an auto parts store, check tire tread and pressure, top up antifreeze and winter washer fluid, and replace worn wipers. Then pack an emergency kit for the trunk in case you’re stranded anyway.

Cold weather lowers a battery’s power just as the engine needs more of it to start, so a battery that starts the car slowly in October can fail on the first frigid morning.

AutoZone and O’Reilly Auto Parts test batteries free, right in the car. Replacing a weak one now costs only a battery you’d soon need anyway, while waiting until it fails adds a jump-start or tow, plus a late start to work.

Where snow and ice are routine, switch to winter tires, which keep their grip below about 45 degrees, when all-season tires stiffen. Your regular tires don’t wear while they’re stored, so they last more seasons. If you’ll keep the car a few years, put the winter set on its own wheels, so each spring and fall swap skips paying a shop to remount the tires. Where snow is occasional, all-weather tires can stay on all year, and unlike most all-season tires they carry the mountain-and-snowflake symbol for severe snow.

Stock the trunk with an ice scraper, a blanket, a flashlight, jumper cables or a jump starter, kitty litter for traction, water, snacks, and a phone charger. Save a roadside assistance number in your phone, and decide whether a AAA membership is worth it.

Drivers stuck in snow should stay with the car, keep the tailpipe clear so fumes can’t back up inside, and run the engine about 10 minutes an hour for heat, with a window cracked.
